Based in Nashville, Tennessee, the company was formed through the 2020 merger of Qualifacts (which offered the CareLogic platform) and Credible Behavioral Health (founded in 2000), orchestrated by private equity firms Warburg Pincus and Martis Capital. Qualifacts subsequently acquired InSync (December 2021) and OnCall Health (November 2022, a Canadian telehealth/patient portal company), assembling a portfolio of four distinct EHR platforms under one umbrella.\n     9→\n    10→The combined company claims over 2,500 customers representing 75,000 providers serving more than 6 million patients across all 50 states. Qualifacts says it serves one-third of participating Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinic (CCBHC) agencies nationally, with over 170 CCBHC customers as of late 2023. Qualifacts was acquired by Warburg Pincus in 2019 for $300M, and later received majority investment from Great Hill Partners. The company holds the #1 ranking in the KLAS Best in Software and Professional Services report for Behavioral Health.\n    11→\n    12→Qualifacts has four ONC-certified EHR platforms: **CareLogic** (enterprise-focused), **Credible** (large agency/enterprise), **InSync** (small-to-mid practice), and **OnCall** (virtual care). Each serves a distinct segment of the behavioral health market.\n    13→\n    14→## Product: Credible Behavioral Health\n    15→\n    16→CHPL ID: 11183\n    17→CHPL Product Number: 15.04.04.3124.Cred.11.01.1.221230\n    18→Version: Version 11\n    19→Certification Date: 2022-12-30\n    20→Certification Status: Active\n    21→\n    22→### What It Is\n    23→\n    24→Credible Behavioral Health is a comprehensive, web-based (cloud-hosted, SaaS) EHR platform designed specifically for behavioral health and human services organizations. It is a full-featured EHR — not a component of a larger product — encompassing clinical documentation, billing, scheduling, treatment planning, ePrescribing, client engagement, analytics, and administrative functions in an integrated platform. The platform has been in use for over 20 years (originally from Credible Behavioral Health Inc., founded 2000, which merged with Qualifacts in 2020).\n    25→\n    26→The certified module covers a broad range of ONC criteria (40+ certified criteria spanning clinical data [(a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14)–(a)(15)], care coordination [(b)(1)–(b)(3), (b)(8), (b)(10)–(b)(11)], patient portal [(e)(1), (e)(3)], clinical quality measures [(c)(1)–(c)(4)], public health reporting [(f)(1)–(f)(2), (f)(5)], and FHIR APIs [(g)(7), (g)(9)–(g)(10)]). The SED intended user description is \"Outpatient Clinic.\"\n    27→\n    28→### Users & Market\n    29→\n    30→Credible targets large enterprise agencies, multi-site behavioral health organizations, and Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinics (CCBHCs). The platform supports community-based outpatient, residential, and inpatient care settings.\n    31→\n    32→**Typical users include:**\n    33→- Therapists, psychiatrists, and behavioral health clinicians\n    34→- Clinical supervisors and program directors\n    35→- Billing staff and revenue cycle managers\n    36→- System administrators\n    37→- Intake coordinators and case managers\n    38→- Clients/patients (via the patient portal)\n    39→\n    40→**Settings served:**\n    41→- Community mental health centers\n    42→- Addiction/substance use treatment programs\n    43→- Intellectual/Developmental Disability (I/DD) services\n    44→- Health and human services agencies\n    45→- Residential treatment facilities\n    46→- County behavioral health departments (notably multiple California counties chose Qualifacts)\n    47→- CCBHCs\n    48→\n    49→Qualifacts claims the Credible platform is ranked #1 in KLAS for Behavioral Health Software and Professional Services. Based in Nashville, Tennessee, the company was formed through the 2020 merger of Qualifacts (which offered the CareLogic platform) and Credible Behavioral Health (founded in 2000), orchestrated by private equity firms Warburg Pincus and Martis Capital. Qualifacts subsequently acquired InSync (December 2021) and OnCall Health (November 2022, a Canadian telehealth/patient portal company), assembling a portfolio of four distinct EHR platforms under one umbrella.\n\nThe combined company claims over 2,500 customers representing 75,000 providers serving more than 6 million patients across all 50 states. Qualifacts says it serves one-third of participating Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinic (CCBHC) agencies nationally, with over 170 CCBHC customers as of late 2023. Qualifacts was acquired by Warburg Pincus in 2019 for $300M, and later received majority investment from Great Hill Partners. The company holds the #1 ranking in the KLAS Best in Software and Professional Services report for Behavioral Health.\n\nQualifacts has four ONC-certified EHR platforms: **CareLogic** (enterprise-focused), **Credible** (large agency/enterprise), **InSync** (small-to-mid practice), and **OnCall** (virtual care). Each serves a distinct segment of the behavioral health market.\n\n## Product: Credible Behavioral Health\n\nCHPL ID: 11183\nCHPL Product Number: 15.04.04.3124.Cred.11.01.1.221230\nVersion: Version 11\nCertification Date: 2022-12-30\nCertification Status: Active\n\n### What It Is\n\nCredible Behavioral Health is a comprehensive, web-based (cloud-hosted, SaaS) EHR platform designed specifically for behavioral health and human services organizations. It is a full-featured EHR — not a component of a larger product — encompassing clinical documentation, billing, scheduling, treatment planning, ePrescribing, client engagement, analytics, and administrative functions in an integrated platform. The platform has been in use for over 20 years (originally from Credible Behavioral Health Inc., founded 2000, which merged with Qualifacts in 2020).\n\nThe certified module covers a broad range of ONC criteria (40+ certified criteria spanning clinical data [(a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14)–(a)(15)], care coordination [(b)(1)–(b)(3), (b)(8), (b)(10)–(b)(11)], patient portal [(e)(1), (e)(3)], clinical quality measures [(c)(1)–(c)(4)], public health reporting [(f)(1)–(f)(2), (f)(5)], and FHIR APIs [(g)(7), (g)(9)–(g)(10)]). The SED intended user description is \"Outpatient Clinic.\"\n\n### Users & Market\n\nCredible targets large enterprise agencies, multi-site behavioral health organizations, and Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinics (CCBHCs). The platform supports community-based outpatient, residential, and inpatient care settings.\n\n**Typical users include:**\n- Therapists, psychiatrists, and behavioral health clinicians\n- Clinical supervisors and program directors\n- Billing staff and revenue cycle managers\n- System administrators\n- Intake coordinators and case managers\n- Clients/patients (via the patient portal)\n\n**Settings served:**\n- Community mental health centers\n- Addiction/substance use treatment programs\n- Intellectual/Developmental Disability (I/DD) services\n- Health and human services agencies\n- Residential treatment facilities\n- County behavioral health departments (notably multiple California counties chose Qualifacts)\n- CCBHCs\n\nQualifacts claims the Credible platform is ranked #1 in KLAS for Behavioral Health Software and Professional Services. An 80% user satisfaction rating was reported based on 96 reviews across major review sites.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on vendor materials and review sites, Credible offers the following major modules and capabilities:\n\n**Clinical Documentation & Assessment**\n- Configurable clinical forms and documentation workflows\n- Integrated assessment tools: Wiley Treatment Planners, CANS (Child and Adolescent Needs and Strengths), ASAM (American Society of Addiction Medicine), PHQ-9, DLA-20, and more\n- Treatment planning with outcome alignment\n- Progress notes / therapy session documentation\n- Clinical decision support with evidence-based treatment suggestions, preventive care reminders, drug interaction alerts\n- 60+ configurable alert types for compliance and quality tracking\n\n**ePrescribing & Medication Management**\n- Electronic prescribing including EPCS (electronic prescribing of controlled substances)\n- PDMP (Prescription Drug Monitoring Program) integration\n- Medication management and adherence tracking\n- Drug interaction checking\n\n**Scheduling & Intake**\n- Appointment scheduling with provider availability/specialty matching\n- Intake workflows\n- Appointment reminders to reduce no-show rates\n- In-person and virtual appointment scheduling\n\n**Billing & Revenue Cycle Management**\n- Integrated HIPAA-compliant billing\n- Claim scrubbing and denial management queues\n- Batch claim error reporting\n- Configurable billing rules for various payment models and value-based care\n- State-specific billing requirements support\n- Revenue cycle management\n- Credentialing services\n- Claims submission to payers\n\n**Residential & Inpatient Management**\n- Facility whiteboard for shift-to-shift consistency\n- Duty log tracking\n- Room assignment and transfer management\n- Census reporting dashboards\n- Virtual representation of inpatient/residential clients\n\n**Client Engagement & Telehealth**\n- HIPAA-compliant, white-labeled patient/client portal\n- Telehealth / video visits supporting groups up to 50 participants (via OnCall integration)\n- Secure messaging\n- Content delivery to clients through the portal\n\n**Analytics & Reporting**\n- Customizable reporting dashboards with graphical capabilities\n- BI Dashboard Library for organizational metrics\n- Advanced search across multiple data fields\n- Reports covering financial performance, staffing, program performance, client outcomes\n- BI Connector for data integration with external systems\n\n**Interoperability & Data Exchange**\n- Connection modules for sending demographic and clinical data to HIEs\n- FHIR R4 API support for 21st Century Cures Act compliance\n- Integration with state HIEs, labs, hospitals, and primary care providers\n- OneQ integration layer for connecting credentialing, MAT, primary care, RCM, BI solutions\n- Over 80 integrations available through the Qualifacts integration marketplace\n- Public health reporting capabilities (immunization registries, syndromic surveillance, electronic case reporting per certified criteria)\n\n**Administration & Security**\n- Self-service system configuration (admin can modify workflows, forms, programs without vendor)\n- SSL encryption, two-factor/multi-factor authentication, IP whitelisting\n- Single Sign-On (SSO) integration\n- Role-based access controls\n- Client data restriction for sensitive accounts\n- SOC2-compliant password policies\n- Auto-save and draft recovery features\n\n**AI-Powered Features**\n- Qualifacts iQ: AI-powered clinical documentation that generates notes from session transcripts\n- Measurement-based care tools\n\n**Mobile Access**\n- HIPAA-compliant mobile application for community-based and field-based care\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the features and modules described, Credible stores and manages the following data categories:\n\n**Clinical Data** (strongly evidenced by certified criteria and vendor materials):\n- Patient/client demographics and contact information\n- Clinical assessments and screening results (PHQ-9, CANS, ASAM, DLA-20, etc.)\n- Treatment plans and goals with outcome tracking\n- Progress notes and session documentation\n- Problem lists, medication lists, medication allergy lists (certified criteria a)(1)–(a)(3))\n- CPOE / clinical orders (certified criterion (a)(4))\n- Clinical decision support data (certified criterion (a)(5))\n- Medication prescriptions (ePrescribing, EPCS)\n- Drug interaction alerts\n- Vitals/vital signs (implied by clinical criteria)\n- Immunization records (certified for immunization registry reporting (f)(1))\n- Lab orders and results (implied by interoperability with labs)\n\n**Administrative & Operational Data**:\n- Scheduling / appointment data\n- Intake records\n- Facility/residential census and room assignments\n- Duty logs and shift records\n- Provider/staff information (specialties, availability, credentials)\n- Program and location configurations\n\n**Billing & Financial Data**:\n- Insurance/payer information\n- Claims data (submissions, denials, scrubbing results)\n- Payment and receivables records\n- Authorization tracking\n- Credentialing records\n\n**Communication & Engagement Data**:\n- Client portal messages and communications\n- Telehealth session records\n- Appointment reminders and notifications\n\n**Reporting & Analytics Data**:\n- Aggregated outcome measures\n- Quality metrics (CQMs per certified criteria)\n- Financial/operational dashboards\n- Syndromic surveillance and public health reporting data\n\n**Interoperability/Exchange Data**:\n- CCD/CCDA documents (transitions of care, per (b)(1)–(b)(3))\n- HIE data exchange records\n- FHIR API access logs\n\n**Notable**: The vendor's website and materials clearly describe integrated billing with claim scrubbing (indicating claims/financial data is stored within the system, not outsourced). The ePrescribing/EPCS capability confirms medication and prescription data. The residential/inpatient management features confirm census, room assignment, and facility operations data. The 60+ alert types and clinical decision support confirm that alert configurations and clinical rules data are stored. id=\"credible-ehi-export\">\n    49→<h1>Credible EHI Export</h1>\n    50→<p><strong>Supported Product:</strong>  Credible Behavioral Health Version 11</p>\n    51→<section id=\"single-patient-export\">\n    52→<h2>Single Patient Export</h2>\n    53→<div class=\"line-block\">\n    54→<div class=\"line\">Patients and their authorized representatives must request the Single Patient export from their provider directly.</div>\n    55→<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n    56→<div class=\"line\">Designated agency staff initiate the Single Patient Export process in the Credible application.</div>\n    57→<div class=\"line\">The request is added to the Single Patient Export queue and processed in the order it was queued.</div>\n    58→<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n    59→<div class=\"line\">Qualifacts staff cannot provide Single Patient EHI Exports on behalf of any agency.</div>\n    60→</div>\n    61→</section>\n    62→<section id=\"patient-population-export\">\n    63→<h2>Patient Population Export</h2>\n    64→<div class=\"line-block\">\n    65→<div class=\"line\">Designated agency staff request the Patient Population Export by submitting a ticket through the Qualifacts Care Center.</div>\n    66→</div>\n    67→<div class=\"line-block\">\n    68→<div class=\"line\">Qualifacts staff will initiate the Patient Population Export process.</div>\n    69→<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n    70→<div class=\"line\">Please allow up to 30 calendar days for the Patient Population Export process to complete.</div>\n    71→</div>\n    72→<div class=\"admonition note\">\n    73→<p class=\"admonition-title\">Note</p>\n    74→<p>Only one Patient Population Export can be requested at a time.\n    75→By requesting a Patient Population Export, you agree to accept and be fully bound by the <a class=\"reference external\" href=\"https://documentation.qualifacts.com/ehi-export/_downloads/Qualifacts_EHI_Export_Terms_of_Use_SEP_2024.pdf\">Qualifacts Terms and Conditions</a>.</p>\n    76→</div>\n    77→</section>\n    78→<section id=\"accessing-the-ehi-export\">\n    79→<h2>Accessing the EHI Export</h2>\n    80→<p>Designated agency staff access completed Single Patient and Patient Population EHI exports directly in Credible through the Report Inbox on the Reports tab.</p>\n    81→<div class=\"admonition note\">\n    82→<p class=\"admonition-title\">Note</p>\n    83→<p>All EHI Export download links automatically expire after 30 calendar days.</p>\n    84→</div>\n    85→</section>\n    86→<section id=\"technical-documentation\">\n    87→<h2>Technical Documentation</h2>\n    88→<section id=\"file-types\">\n    89→<h3>File Types</h3>\n    90→<div class=\"line-block\">\n    91→<div class=\"line\">The EHI Export consists of a zip file containing a set of Newline Delimited JSON files.</div>\n    92→<div class=\"line\">Each JSON file represents a subset of Electronic Health Information, organized around data classes.</div>\n    93→<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n    94→<div class=\"line\">Attachments hosted on Credible servers are provided in the original uploaded file type with a GUID file name for matching with the ContentAttachments.json information.</div>\n    95→<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n    96→<div class=\"line\">For Partner agencies using their own file storage, the file’s path will be shown in the ContentAttachments.json.  The Partner agency must be contacted to obtain the actual file.</div>\n    97→</div>\n    98→<p>Sample ContentAttachments.json showing both Credible-hosted and remote files.</p>\n    99→<blockquote>\n id=\"credible-ehi-export\">\n<h1>Credible EHI Export</h1>\n<p><strong>Supported Product:</strong>  Credible Behavioral Health Version 11</p>\n<section id=\"single-patient-export\">\n<h2>Single Patient Export</h2>\n<div class=\"line-block\">\n<div class=\"line\">Patients and their authorized representatives must request the Single Patient export from their provider directly.</div>\n<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n<div class=\"line\">Designated agency staff initiate the Single Patient Export process in the Credible application.</div>\n<div class=\"line\">The request is added to the Single Patient Export queue and processed in the order it was queued.</div>\n<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n<div class=\"line\">Qualifacts staff cannot provide Single Patient EHI Exports on behalf of any agency.</div>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section id=\"patient-population-export\">\n<h2>Patient Population Export</h2>\n<div class=\"line-block\">\n<div class=\"line\">Designated agency staff request the Patient Population Export by submitting a ticket through the Qualifacts Care Center.</div>\n</div>\n<div class=\"line-block\">\n<div class=\"line\">Qualifacts staff will initiate the Patient Population Export process.</div>\n<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n<div class=\"line\">Please allow up to 30 calendar days for the Patient Population Export process to complete.</div>\n</div>\n<div class=\"admonition note\">\n<p class=\"admonition-title\">Note</p>\n<p>Only one Patient Population Export can be requested at a time.\nBy requesting a Patient Population Export, you agree to accept and be fully bound by the <a class=\"reference external\" href=\"https://documentation.qualifacts.com/ehi-export/_downloads/Qualifacts_EHI_Export_Terms_of_Use_SEP_2024.pdf\">Qualifacts Terms and Conditions</a>.</p>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section id=\"accessing-the-ehi-export\">\n<h2>Accessing the EHI Export</h2>\n<p>Designated agency staff access completed Single Patient and Patient Population EHI exports directly in Credible through the Report Inbox on the Reports tab.</p>\n<div class=\"admonition note\">\n<p class=\"admonition-title\">Note</p>\n<p>All EHI Export download links automatically expire after 30 calendar days.</p>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section id=\"technical-documentation\">\n<h2>Technical Documentation</h2>\n<section id=\"file-types\">\n<h3>File Types</h3>\n<div class=\"line-block\">\n<div class=\"line\">The EHI Export consists of a zip file containing a set of Newline Delimited JSON files.</div>\n<div class=\"line\">Each JSON file represents a subset of Electronic Health Information, organized around data classes.</div>\n<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n<div class=\"line\">Attachments hosted on Credible servers are provided in the original uploaded file type with a GUID file name for matching with the ContentAttachments.json information.</div>\n<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n<div class=\"line\">For Partner agencies using their own file storage, the file’s path will be shown in the ContentAttachments.json.  The Partner agency must be contacted to obtain the actual file.</div>\n</div>\n<p>Sample ContentAttachments.json showing both Credible-hosted and remote files.</p>\n<blockquote>\n<div><div Requests enter a processing queue.\n2. **Patient Population Export**: Requested by submitting a ticket through the Qualifacts Care Center. Qualifacts staff initiate the process. May take up to 30 calendar days. Only one can be requested at a time. Requires agreement to Terms of Use.\n\nCompleted exports are accessed via the **Report Inbox** on the Reports tab within Credible. Download links expire after 30 calendar days.\n\n### Export Format\nThe export is a **ZIP file containing Newline Delimited JSON (NDJSON) files**. Each JSON file represents a data class (entity), with the naming convention `Content{EntityName}.json`. For example:\n- `ContentClaims.json` — claims data\n- `ContentAllergies.json` — allergy records\n- `ContentVisitService.json` — visit/service records\n- `ContentClientProfile_Custom.json` — custom profile fields\n\n**Attachments** (documents, images) are included in their original uploaded file format with GUID filenames that map to records in `ContentClientAttachments.json`. For partner agencies using their own file storage, the file path is provided but the actual file must be obtained from the partner.\n\n### Data Dictionary Scope\nThe data dictionary documents **106 entities** with **1,993 fields** across the following domains:\n\n**Clinical Data (15 entities, 280 fields)**:\nAllergies, Client Diagnosis, Diagnosis Detail, Immunizations, Implantable Device, The export covers:\n\n**Clearly Covered:**\n- **Demographics & contacts**: Profile, Profile Extended, Profile Previous Address/FullName, Contact, Family\n- **Diagnoses**: Client Diagnosis, Diagnosis Detail, Visit Service Diagnosis (3 separate entities with 92 combined fields)\n- **Medications & prescribing**: Medication Request, Medication History, EMAR, eRx Messages (10 entities, 293 fields — fields)\n- **Clinical notes & documentation**: Clinical Notes, Notes, Credible Plan Documentation\n- **Treatment planning**: Treatment Plan, Treatment Plan Plus (with Details and Extended), Credible Assessment, Questionnaire, Questionnaire Category, Outcomes\n- **Billing & claims**: Claims, Payments, Payment Plan, Liability (164 fields!), Statement Header/Detail, 277 Response — this is extensive billing coverage\n- **Insurance/enrollment**: Insurance (50 fields), Insurance Subscriber, Eligibility, Enrollment, Payer, Insurance Visit Type\n- **Authorization**: Authorization, Authorization Provider, Authorization Visit Type, Medication Prior Authorization\n- **Visit/service records**: Visit Service (97 fields), Visit Service Approval, Visit Service Claim Note, Visit Service Diagnosis, ContentClientAttachments with GUID mapping to actual files\n- **Care coordination**: Care Team, Care Plan, Direct Sent/Received Messages\n- **Family medical history**: Family Medical History, Family Medical History Detail (66 combined fields)\n- **Implantable devices**: 20-field entity\n- **Scheduling**: Scheduler Appointment, Scheduler Planner\n- **Custom agency-specific data**: Through custom fields on Profile, Episode, Medical Profile, Visit Service, Employee\n\n**Potential gaps (minor):**\n- **Vital signs**: No dedicated \"Vitals\" entity is documented. Vitals may be captured within Visit Service custom fields or Questionnaire data, but there's no explicit vitals entity. For a behavioral health system, this is a minor gap — vitals are tracked less frequently than in primary care.\n- **Telehealth session records**: No explicit telehealth entity, though Qualifacts added OnCall Health for telehealth. These may be captured as Visit Service records.\n- **AI-generated content**: Qualifacts iQ (AI note generation) content may be captured in Clinical Notes, but there's no explicit entity for AI transcripts or generated content.\n\n### (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Assessment\n\n**This is a genuine (b)(10) export, not a repackaged FHIR API.** Key indicators:\n1. The export uses **NDJSON files in a vendor-defined schema**, not FHIR resources\n2. The data dictionary contains **106 entities with 1,993 fields** — far beyond US Core's ~20 resource types\n3. **Billing data is extensively covered**: Claims, Payments, Liability (164 fields), Statements, 277 Response, Funding Activity\n4. **Behavioral health specialty data**: ASAM Assessment, Credible Plans, Treatment Plan Plus, Questionnaires, Bed Board data — none of which map to standard FHIR US Core\n5. **Custom fields are exported**: Agency-configurable fields are included in the export\n6. The export format uses SQL-style data types (varchar, decimal, smallint) suggesting a database-level export rather than FHIR API output\n7. The FHIR API is mentioned separately on the main index page as a distinct capability\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export is a proprietary **NDJSON-based format** with human-readable field names. Each entity maps to a JSON file. This is not a recognized standard (not FHIR, C-CDA, or HL7 v2), but it's well-documented and structured.\n\n**Strengths of the format:**\n- Human-readable field names (e.g., \"Insurance Policy Number\", not cryptic codes)\n- JSON is widely parseable\n- Each data class in its own file enables incremental processing\n- Attachment files are included with GUID-based mapping\n- Custom fields are included per agency configuration\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- Relationships between entities rely on shared ID fields (Client Id, Visit Id, Claim Id, etc.) but there's no explicit relationship documentation or foreign key map\n- No formal schema (no JSON Schema, no OpenAPI spec) — just the data dictionary\n- Date format inconsistency: some fields use \"2023-09-06T10:54:00\", others use \"5/3/2021 2:18:00 PM\"\n- No explicit documentation of value sets for coded fields (e.g., what are the valid values for \"Current Status\" in Claims?)\n- The description field often just restates the field name (e.g., field \"Batch Date\" described as \"Batch Date\") rather than explaining semantics\n\n**Could a third party reconstruct the patient record?** Mostly yes. The field names are descriptive enough that a competent developer could import the data. The main challenges would be:\n1. Understanding the relationships between entities (e.g., how Visit Service relates to Claims)\n2. Interpreting coded fields without value set documentation\n3. Handling the mixed date formats\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- Purpose-built Angular SPA data dictionary with clean navigation\n- Every entity has example JSON showing exact output format\n- Field-level documentation with data types and nullability\n- Version-stamped (V20251217.1) suggesting active maintenance\n- 106 entities covering clinical, billing, administrative, residential, and behavioral health data\n- Custom fields acknowledged and their handling explained\n- Attachment handling documented with sample showing both hosted and remote files\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- Descriptions are often tautological (field name = description)\n- No value set/code system documentation for coded fields\n- No entity relationship documentation\n- No explicit schema files (JSON Schema, XSD, etc.)\n- No documentation of how the export handles deleted/amended records beyond the \"Deleted\" boolean field on Claims\n- No sample export ZIP file provided for developers to work with\n- No explicit documentation of NDJSON line-by-line format expectations (though example files clarify this)\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\nThe field-level documentation is moderately granular:\n- Field names: yes\n- Data types: yes (SQL-style)\n- Nullability: yes\n- Example values: yes\n- Descriptions: yes, but often minimal\n- Value sets for coded fields: no\n- Cardinality/relationships: no\n- Versioning: yes (V20251217.1)\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://documentation.qualifacts.com/ehi-export/\n- Credible-specific page: https://documentation.qualifacts.com/ehi-export/platform/credible/credible-ehi-export.html\n- Data Dictionary: https://b10export-docs.cbh4.crediblebh.com/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: yes (data dictionary is Angular SPA; Assessment, Questionnaire, Questionnaire Category, Outcomes\n- **Billing & claims**: Claims, Payments, Payment Plan, Liability (164 fields!), Statement Header/Detail, 277 Response — this is extensive billing coverage\n- **Insurance/enrollment**: Insurance (50 fields), Insurance Subscriber, Eligibility, Enrollment, Payer, Insurance Visit Type\n- **Authorization**: Authorization, Authorization Provider, Authorization Visit Type, Medication Prior Authorization\n- **Visit/service records**: Visit Service (97 fields), Visit Service Approval, Visit Service Claim Note, Visit Service Diagnosis, Visit Service Insurance, Visit Service Transportation\n- **Residential/inpatient**: Bed Board Billing, Bed Board Shift Notes, Bed Board Whiteboard Notes, Foster Home\n- **Attachments**: ContentClientAttachments with GUID mapping to actual files\n- **Care coordination**: Care Team, Care Plan, Direct Sent/Received Messages\n- **Family medical history**: Family Medical History, Family Medical History Detail (66 combined fields)\n- **Implantable devices**: 20-field entity\n- **Scheduling**: Scheduler Appointment, Scheduler Planner\n- **Custom agency-specific data**: Through custom fields on Profile, Episode, Medical Profile, Visit Service, Employee\n\n**Potential gaps (minor):**\n- **Vital signs**: No dedicated \"Vitals\" entity is documented. Vitals may be captured within Visit Service custom fields or Questionnaire data, but there's no explicit vitals entity. For a behavioral health system, this is a minor gap — vitals are tracked less frequently than in primary care.\n- **Telehealth session records**: No explicit telehealth entity, though Qualifacts added OnCall Health for telehealth. These may be captured as Visit Service records.\n- **AI-generated content**: Qualifacts iQ (AI note generation) content may be captured in Clinical Notes, but there's no explicit entity for AI transcripts or generated content.\n\n### (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Assessment\n\n**This is a genuine (b)(10) export, not a repackaged FHIR API.** Key indicators:\n1. The export uses **NDJSON files in a vendor-defined schema**, not FHIR resources\n2. The data dictionary contains **106 entities with 1,993 fields** — far beyond US Core's ~20 resource types\n3. **Billing data is extensively covered**: Claims, Payments, Liability (164 fields), Statements, 277 Response, Funding Activity\n4. **Behavioral health specialty data**: ASAM Assessment, Credible Plans, Treatment Plan Plus, Questionnaires, Bed Board data — none of which map to standard FHIR US Core\n5. **Custom fields are exported**: Agency-configurable fields are included in the export\n6. The export format uses SQL-style data types (varchar, decimal, smallint) suggesting a database-level export rather than FHIR API output\n7. The FHIR API is mentioned separately on the main index page as a distinct capability\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export is a proprietary **NDJSON-based format** with human-readable field names. Each entity maps to a JSON file. This is not a recognized standard (not FHIR, C-CDA, or HL7 v2), but it's well-documented and structured.\n\n**Strengths of the format:**\n- Human-readable field names (e.g., \"Insurance Policy Number\", not cryptic codes)\n- JSON is widely parseable\n- Each data class in its own file enables incremental processing\n- Attachment files are included with GUID-based mapping\n- Custom fields are included per agency configuration\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- Relationships between entities rely on shared ID fields (Client Id, Visit Id, Claim Id, etc.) but there's no explicit relationship documentation or foreign key map\n- No formal schema (no JSON Schema, no OpenAPI spec) — just the data dictionary\n- Date format inconsistency: some fields use \"2023-09-06T10:54:00\", others use \"5/3/2021 2:18:00 PM\"\n- No explicit documentation of value sets for coded fields (e.g., what are the valid values for \"Current Status\" in Claims?)\n- The description field often just restates the field name (e.g., field \"Batch Date\" described as \"Batch Date\") rather than explaining semantics\n\n**Could a third party reconstruct the patient record?** Mostly yes. The field names are descriptive enough that a competent developer could import the data. The main challenges would be:\n1. Understanding the relationships between entities (e.g., how Visit Service relates to Claims)\n2. Interpreting coded fields without value set documentation\n3. Handling the mixed date formats\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- Purpose-built Angular SPA data dictionary with clean navigation\n- Every entity has example JSON showing exact output format\n- Field-level documentation with data types and nullability\n- Version-stamped (V20251217.1) suggesting active maintenance\n- 106 entities covering clinical, billing, administrative, residential, and behavioral health data\n- Custom fields acknowledged and their handling explained\n- Attachment handling documented with sample showing both hosted and remote files\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- Descriptions are often tautological (field name = description)\n- No value set/code system documentation for coded fields\n- No entity relationship documentation\n- No explicit schema files (JSON Schema, XSD, etc.)\n- No documentation of how the export handles deleted/amended records beyond the \"Deleted\" boolean field on Claims\n- No sample export ZIP file provided for developers to work with\n- No explicit documentation of NDJSON line-by-line format expectations (though example files clarify this)\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\nThe field-level documentation is moderately granular:\n- Field names: yes\n- Data types: yes (SQL-style)\n- Nullability: yes\n- Example values: yes\n- Descriptions: yes, but often minimal\n- Value sets for coded fields: no\n- Cardinality/relationships: no\n- Versioning: yes (V20251217.1)\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://documentation.qualifacts.com/ehi-export/\n- Credible-specific page: https://documentation.qualifacts.com/ehi-export/platform/credible/credible-ehi-export.html\n- Data Dictionary: https://b10export-docs.cbh4.crediblebh.com/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: yes (data dictionary is Angular SPA; main pages work with curl)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (from index to Credible page, then link to data dictionary)\n- Anti-bot issues: Cloudflare on main site, no issues; data dictionary on CloudFront, no issues\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- The data dictionary is an Angular SPA, so `curl` returns only the shell HTML with `<app-root></app-root>`. All entity data is compiled into the JavaScript bundle (`main.3a772189aaaeff56.js`). A browser is required to view the data dictionary interactively.\n- Successfully extracted all 106 entities and 1,993 fields from the JS bundle programmatically, providing a machine-readable `data-dictionary.json` that the SPA itself does not offer as a download.\n- No download/export button exists on the data dictionary site — the only way to get the data in bulk is to extract it from the JavaScript bundle or scrape each page individually.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
